         <section class="article-section__sub-content" id="smll73095-sec-0240">
            
            <h3 class="article-section__sub-title section2" id="smll73095-sec-0240-title">4.10 Transfection Efficiency</h3>
            
            <p>CHO were seeded in a 6-well plate at a density of 150&nbsp;000 cells per well for a minimal time of 24&nbsp;h and until it reaches ≥80% confluency, which corresponds to approximately 0.8–1.0 × 10<sup>6</sup> cells. The plate was incubated for 24&nbsp;h in the incubator (37°C, 5% CO<sub>2</sub>, 95% humidity). After washing with 1&nbsp;mL PBS, 1&nbsp;mL of serum-free DMEM containing various amounts of mRNA-LNPs (10, 20, 50, 100, 200, and 500&nbsp;ng mRNA per 10<sup>5</sup> cells) was added to each well. After 24&nbsp;h, cells were imaged by fluorescence microscopy (Olympus IX-81) using a 488&nbsp;nm excitation laser for eGFP. Fluorescence images were processed using Fiji [<span><a href="#smll73095-bib-0072" class="bibLink tab-link" id="smll73095-bib-0072_R_d140344236e1883" data-tab="pane-pcw-references" aria-label="Reference 72 - Nature Methods">72</a></span>]. Brightness and contrast adjustments were applied using linear scaling and were kept identical across all images within each experiment with no further transformations or corrections applied. Cells were then detached with trypsin, pelleted, resuspended in 300&nbsp;µL PBS, and analyzed for eGFP expression by flow cytometry (BD Fortessa). FlowJo software was used for data processing.</p>
            </section>
         
         <section class="article-section__sub-content" id="smll73095-sec-0250">
            
            <h3 class="article-section__sub-title section2" id="smll73095-sec-0250-title">4.11 In Vitro Immune Response</h3>
            
            <p>SVEC4-10 cells were cultured and transfected following the methods in Section&nbsp;<a class="sectionLink scrollableLink" href="#smll73095-sec-0240">4.10</a>. SVEC4-10 cells were seeded in a 6-well plate until ≥80% confluency. After washing with 1&nbsp;mL PBS, 1&nbsp;mL of serum-free DMEM containing mRNA-LNPs with 200&nbsp;ng mRNA per 10<sup>5</sup> cells was added to each well. The plate was incubated for 24&nbsp;h in the incubator (37°C, 5% CO<sub>2</sub>, 95% humidity). Cells (1 × 10<sup>6</sup> cells) were processed 24&nbsp;h post-transfection by lysing in 1&nbsp;mL TRIzol Reagent. After 5&nbsp;min incubation, 200 µL chloroform was added per 1&nbsp;mL TRIzol, and samples were shaken vigorously, then centrifuged at 15,000×g for 15&nbsp;min at 4°C. The upper aqueous phase was transferred to a new tube, mixed 1:1 with isopropanol, and centrifuged at 15,000×g for 10&nbsp;min at 4°C. RNA pellets were washed three times with 75% ethanol, air-dried, and dissolved in 200 µL of RNase-free water. Purity and concentration of extracted RNA were measured via NanoDrop (optimal A260/A280 ratio ∼2). cDNA synthesis and qPCR were carried out using the Bio-Rad One-Step Kit in a 384-well format (MicroAmp EnduraPlate Optical 384-Well). Primers for pro-inflammatory cytokines (IL-6), adhesion molecules (VCAM-1, ICAM-1), and anti-inflammatory cytokines (e.g., IL-10) were selected to assess immune responses. The Applied Biosystems QuantStudio 7 platform was used with GAPDH as the internal reference gene. Relative expression levels were determined using the 2^−ΔΔCt method.</p>
            </section>
         
         <section class="article-section__sub-content" id="smll73095-sec-0260">
            
            <h3 class="article-section__sub-title section2" id="smll73095-sec-0260-title">4.12 In Vivo Biodistribution Studies</h3>
            
            <section class="article-section__sub-content" id="smll73095-sec-0270">
               
               <h4 class="article-section__sub-title section3" id="smll73095-sec-0270-title">4.12.1 Animal Experiments</h4>
               
               <p>All animal experiments were approved by the Alfred Plus Alliance Animal Ethics Committee (No. E/8196/2025/B and E/10630/2024/B) and conducted in accordance with institutional guidelines at the Baker Heart and Diabetes Institute, Melbourne, Australia. C57BL/6 wild-type mice (20–25 g, approximately equal numbers of males and females) were obtained from the Alfred Medical Research and Education Precinct Animal Services. B6.Cg-Gt(ROSA)26Sortm9(CAG-tdTomato)Hze/J (Ai9) mice (8–10 weeks old, approximately equal numbers of males and females) were purchased from Jackson Laboratory and bred at the Alfred Medical Research and Education Precinct Animal Facility (AMREP). Mice were randomly assigned to treatment or control groups in a blinded manner. All experimental procedures, including dosing and outcome assessments, were performed under blinded conditions.</p>
               </section>
            
            <section class="article-section__sub-content" id="smll73095-sec-0280">
               
               <h4 class="article-section__sub-title section3" id="smll73095-sec-0280-title">4.12.2 Preliminary Experiments with mCherry mRNA-Loaded OryKL</h4>
               
               <p>Mixed-sex C57BL/6 mice received either PBS control or mCherry mRNA loaded-LNPs via IP and IV injection. After 24&nbsp;h, all mice were euthanized&nbsp;via IP injection of ketamine/xylazine (300 and 50&nbsp;mg kg<sup>−1</sup>, respectively), followed by liver excision. Liver samples were then processed for flow cytometry analysis (mCherry expression at 561&nbsp;nm).</p>
               </section>
            
            <section class="article-section__sub-content" id="smll73095-sec-0290">
               
               <h4 class="article-section__sub-title section3" id="smll73095-sec-0290-title">4.12.3 Organ-Level Biodistribution Studies using NanoLuc mRNA Reporter System</h4>
               
               <p>Mixed-sex C57BL/6 mice received either OryKL encapsulating 10&nbsp;µg NanoLuc mRNA or an equivalent volume of PBS via IV administration, corresponding to a dose of 0.5&nbsp;mg kg<sup>−1</sup>. After 24&nbsp;h, mice received 100&nbsp;µL (0.46&nbsp;µmol) IP injection of Nano-Glo Fluorofurimazine In vivo substrate (Promega), freshly prepared in 1&nbsp;mL sterile PBS. After 15&nbsp;min, all mice were euthanized&nbsp;via IP injection of ketamine/xylazine. Major body organs (liver, spleen, lungs, kidneys, and heart) were collected for further analysis. The whole organ fluorescence was measured using an IVIS Lumina III imaging system and quantified using LivingImage Software.</p>
               </section>
            
            <section class="article-section__sub-content" id="smll73095-sec-0300">
               
               <h4 class="article-section__sub-title section3" id="smll73095-sec-0300-title">4.12.4 Organ- and Cell-Level Biodistribution in Ai9 Mice</h4>
               
               <p>Mixed-sex Ai9 mice were intravenously injected with Cre-recombinase mRNA-loaded LNPs at 0.5&nbsp;mg kg<sup>−1</sup> mRNA dose via tail vein injection, or PBS as a negative control. After 72&nbsp;h, mice were humanely killed using IP injection of pentobarbitone (300&nbsp;mg kg<sup>−1</sup>). Blood and major body organs (liver, spleen, lungs, kidneys, lymph nodes, and heart) were collected for further analysis. The whole organ fluorescence was measured using an IVIS Lumina III imaging system and quantified using LivingImage Software.</p>
               
               <p>Blood samples were treated with red blood cells (RBCs) lysis buffer for 5&nbsp;min on ice and then blood cells were washed in ice-cold PBS via centrifugation at 400&nbsp;g for 5&nbsp;min. Single-cell suspensions of lymph nodes and spleens were obtained using 70-µm filters. Splenic cells were treated with RBCs lysis buffer for 5&nbsp;min and then washed in ice-cold PBS as mentioned before. Liver tissue was minced using a sterile scalpel blade and digested in a 100&nbsp;µg mL<sup>−1</sup> solution of Liberase at 37°C for 1&nbsp;h. After neutralization with 10% FBS-containing buffer, supernatant was removed using centrifugation at 400&nbsp;g for 5&nbsp;min. Cells were resuspended in RBCs lysis buffer for another 5&nbsp;min at 4°C, then washed and passed through a 40-µm filter to obtain single cell suspension.</p>
               
               <p>Single cell suspensions were stained with a panel of anti-mouse antibodies (see Section&nbsp;<a class="sectionLink scrollableLink" href="#smll73095-sec-0150">4.1</a>) at 1:300 dilution for the following markers: CD45, CD11b, CD11c, CD3, CD19, and CD14 or CD68. Data was acquired using a Cytek Aurora flow cytometer and analyzed using SpectroFlo Software. Cell populations were identified as: immune cells (CD45<sup>+</sup>), myeloid cells (CD45<sup>+</sup> CD11b<sup>+</sup>), dendritic cells (CD45<sup>+</sup> CD11b<sup>+</sup> CD11c<sup>+</sup>), macrophages (CD45<sup>+</sup> CD11b<sup>+</sup> CD68<sup>+</sup>), monocytes (CD45<sup>+</sup> CD11b<sup>+</sup> CD14<sup>+</sup>), T cells (CD45<sup>+</sup> CD11b<sup>−</sup> CD3<sup>+</sup>), B cells (CD45<sup>+</sup> CD11b<sup>−</sup> CD19<sup>+</sup>), and liver cells as (CD45<sup>−</sup> population in liver single cell suspensions). Gating strategy for the flow cytometry experiments is shown in Figure <a class="suppLink scrollableLink" href="#support-information-section">S17</a>.</p>
               </section>
            </section>
         
         <section class="article-section__sub-content" id="smll73095-sec-0310">
            
            <h3 class="article-section__sub-title section2" id="smll73095-sec-0310-title">4.13 In Vivo Biosafety</h3>
            
            <section class="article-section__sub-content" id="smll73095-sec-0320">
               
               <h4 class="article-section__sub-title section3" id="smll73095-sec-0320-title">4.13.1 Immune Response by qPCR</h4>
               
               <p>Briefly, ∼25&nbsp;mg liver tissue samples from Section&nbsp;<a class="sectionLink scrollableLink" href="#smll73095-sec-0300">4.12.4</a> were lysed in 1&nbsp;mL TRIzol Reagent. Subsequent RNA extraction, and qPCR analysis were performed following the same procedures described in Section&nbsp;<a class="sectionLink scrollableLink" href="#smll73095-sec-0250">4.11</a>. Gene expression of pro-inflammatory cytokines (IL-1β, IL-6, TNF-α, IFN-γ), adhesion molecules (e.g., ICAM-1), and anti-inflammatory cytokines (e.g., IL-10) was quantified using the Applied Biosystems QuantStudio 7 platform, with GAPDH as the internal reference gene. Relative expression levels were determined using the 2^−ΔΔCt method.</p>
               </section>
            
            <section class="article-section__sub-content" id="smll73095-sec-0330">
               
               <h4 class="article-section__sub-title section3" id="smll73095-sec-0330-title">4.13.2 Histological Analysis</h4>
               
               <p>Mouse tissues (from Section&nbsp;<a class="sectionLink scrollableLink" href="#smll73095-sec-0300">4.12.4</a>) were harvested and embedded in OCT (Tissue-Tek, Sakura Finetek, USA), cryopreserved on dry ice, and stored at −80°C. The tissues were then cryosectioned 6&nbsp;mm thickness onto superfrost glass slide using Epredia HM525NX Cryostat. For H&amp;E staining, the tissue sections were equilibrated to room temperature for 30&nbsp;min, followed by a 5&nbsp;min incubation in PBS (Invitrogen, USA) to remove OCT. The sections were then fixed in formalin solution (Sigma-Aldrich, USA) for 4&nbsp;min, then washed in deionized water for 4&nbsp;min. The tissues were stained with Harris Haematoxylin solution (Amber Scientific, Australian Biostain, South Australia) solution for 10 s and then rinsed in tap water until the water becomes colorless. The tissues were then incubated in Scott's tap water for 10 s and rinsed in running tap water for 5&nbsp;min. For Eosin staining, tissues were incubated in Eosin 1% Alcoholic solution (Amber Scientific, Australian Biostain, South Australia) for 2&nbsp;min. To differentiate the staining, the tissue sections were then dipped 10 times in 95% ethanol, followed by dehydration in 100% ethanol twice for 3&nbsp;min, and two rinses in xylene (Sigma-Aldrich, USA), each for 5&nbsp;min. Finally, the slides were coverslipped using DPX Mountant (Sigma-Aldrich, USA) and then imaged using an Olympus BX43 Microscope at 200× magnification.</p>
               </section>
            </section>
         
         <section class="article-section__sub-content" id="smll73095-sec-0340">
            
            <h3 class="article-section__sub-title section2" id="smll73095-sec-0340-title">4.14 Storage Stability Test</h3>
            
            <p>mRNA-LNPs formulations (OryKL and KC 12 LNPs) were synthesized, diluted in PBS (No sugar, N), 20% w/v sucrose (SU), or 20% w/v trehalose (TR), aliquoted into 1.5&nbsp;mL Eppendorf tubes or 2.0&nbsp;mL cryo-vials, and then subjected to three storage conditions: (1) 4°C (F) storage—only PBS diluted samples; (2) liquid nitrogen (LN) storage—all samples; and (3) −20°C for samples after lyophilization (FD). Prior to size measurements and transactions, LN-stored samples were first rapidly thawed in a 25°C water bath, while lyophilized samples were reconstituted in deionized water. Transfection efficiency (Section&nbsp;<a class="sectionLink scrollableLink" href="#smll73095-sec-0240">4.10</a>) was assessed using CHO cells with the characteristics of mRNA-LNPs measured at one and two months of storage under these conditions.</p>
            </section>
         
         <section class="article-section__sub-content" id="smll73095-sec-0350">
            
            <h3 class="article-section__sub-title section2" id="smll73095-sec-0350-title">4.15 SANS Measurements</h3>
            
            <div class="paragraph-element">SANS experiments were performed using the Bilby SANS [<span><a href="#smll73095-bib-0073" class="bibLink tab-link" id="smll73095-bib-0073_R_d140344236e2063" data-tab="pane-pcw-references" aria-label="Reference 73 - Journal of Applied Crystallography">73</a></span>] beam line at Australian Centre for Neutron Scattering, Australian Nuclear Science and Technology Organisation (ANSTO), Lucas Heights, Australia. OryKL and KC 12 LNPs, prepared as described in Section&nbsp;<a class="sectionLink scrollableLink" href="#smll73095-sec-0220">4.8</a>, were dialyzed in 100&nbsp;mM PBS (pH 7.4) made with deuterated water to reduce background signals and establish a strong contrast between the fully hydrogenous LNP components and the deuterated dispersing medium. For SANS measurements, the sample dispersions (final lipid concentratio<i>n</i> = 4&nbsp;mg mL<sup>−1</sup>) were loaded into 300&nbsp;µL Hellma cells with a 1&nbsp;mm path length and mounted in a 16-position sample changer. SANS measurements were conducted in time-of-flight mode with the incident neutron wavelength of 3–18 Å at ambient temperature (20°C). The raw scattering counts were collected on the main detector at a sample–detector distance of 18 m, combined with a pair of curtain detectors at 1.5 and 2.5 m. The data were reduced using the Mantid package [<span><a href="#smll73095-bib-0074" class="bibLink tab-link" id="smll73095-bib-0074_R_d140344236e2073" data-tab="pane-pcw-references" aria-label="Reference 74 - Nuclear Instruments and Methods in Physics Research Section A: Accelerators, Spectrometers, Detectors and Associated Equipment">74</a></span>], resulting in radially averaged intensity data <i>I</i>(<i>q</i>), where the scattering vector <i>q</i> is defined as:

               <div class="inline-equation" id="smll73095-disp-0004"><span class="inline-equation__construct"><span><!--FIGURE-->
                        <span class="fallback__mathEquation" data-altimg="/cms/asset/b603fc97-944a-4c89-9a6c-098a4c8c48ca/smll73095-math-0004.png"></span><math altimg="urn:x-wiley:16136810:media:smll73095:smll73095-math-0004" display="block" location="graphic/smll73095-math-0004.png">
                           <semantics>
                              <mrow>
                                 <mi>q</mi>
                                 <mo linebreak="badbreak">=</mo>
                                 <mfrac>
                                    <mrow>
                                       <mn>4</mn>
                                       <mi>π</mi>
                                    </mrow>
                                    <mi>λ</mi>
                                 </mfrac>
                                 <mi>sin</mi>
                                 <mfrac>
                                    <mi>θ</mi>
                                    <mn>2</mn>
                                 </mfrac>
                              </mrow>
                              <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">$$\begin{equation}q = \frac{{4\pi }}{\lambda }\sin \frac{\theta }{2}\end{equation}$$</annotation>
                           </semantics></math></span></span><span class="inline-equation__label">(4)</span></div>with λ the incident neutron wavelength and θ the scattering angle. Absolute intensity scaling was achieved based on an empty beam transmission measurement, and the simultaneous <i>q</i> range was 0.001–0.84 Å<sup>−1</sup>. For background subtraction, an empty cell measurement was used. The terms “intensity” or “scattering intensity” in this work refer to the macroscopic differential scattering cross-section or volume-specific differential scattering cross-section (denoted as dΣ/dΩ), with the sample dispersion volume as the reference volume. All background-subtracted SANS data were fitted using core–shell sphere, power-law (for power-law exponent changes in the intermediate <i>q</i> range), and Gaussian model (for the high-<i>q</i> feature in each pattern) using SasView software (<a href="https://www.sasview.org" class="linkBehavior">https://www.sasview.org</a>).</div>
            </section>
         
         <section class="article-section__sub-content" id="smll73095-sec-0360">
            
            <h3 class="article-section__sub-title section2" id="smll73095-sec-0360-title">4.16 Statistical Analysis</h3>
            
            <p>All experimental data were analyzed and plotted using GraphPad Prism (version 10.1.2). Grubb's test was used to detect and exclude outliers. For comparisons between two groups (e.g., one LNP formulation versus control), an unpaired two-tailed <i>t</i>-test was used. For experiments involving more than two groups, a one-way analysis of variance (ANOVA) followed by Tukey's multiple comparisons test was conducted to determine specific group differences relative to negative control. For experiments involving two independent variables, such as LNP type and concentration, a two-way ANOVA with Geisser-Greenhouse correction was employed to evaluate the main effects and potential interaction between variables. Tukey's post hoc test was used for multiple comparisons. <i>p</i>-value was set at 0.05 with significance levels denoted as: *<i>p</i> &lt; 0.05; **<i>p</i> &lt; 0.01; ***<i>p</i> &lt; 0.001; ****<i>p</i> &lt; 0.0001.</p>
            </section>
         </section>
      <div class="article-section__content">
